As orders failed to materialize and the reality of fewer steady projects set in, his mood shifted; he began turning down salsa nights and community events, feeling that his plans had been foolish, and he found it harder to keep up with the bookkeeping and scheduling that kept the business afloat. After nearly two months of this downturn—during which his wife noticed he was talking more about death and seemed less engaged even while cooking or mentoring youth—she convinced him to see a psychiatrist recommended by their parish priest.\n- Chief Complaint: Lately I keep thinking my family might be better off without me, and those thoughts are starting to scare me.\n▸ Patient-specific Symptom Manifestations\n- S024 (Depressed_Mood): Most days lately I wake up with this heavy, sinking feeling in my chest, like something broke inside me when the contracts fell through, and no matter what I do in the shop or at home I just feel sad and hollow, like I’m watching my own life from the outside.\n- S025 (Loss_of_Interest): I used to get excited planning a new table design or going out dancing with my wife on Fridays, but now even when the guys call about salsa night or I’m in front of a beautiful piece of wood, I just feel nothing and end up making excuses to stay home and sit on the couch.\n- S028 (Psychomotor_Changes): My wife tells me I move like I’m walking through mud now, and I notice I just stand there staring at the tools for a long time before I can make myself pick sweeping the shop floor or driving to the lumber yard wears me out the way a full day of heavy lifting used to.\n- S030 (Worthlessness_Guilt): I keep going over every choice I made with the business and beating myself up, telling myself I’m a failure as a provider and that my wife and kids ended up with a useless man who ruined what could have been a good life for them.\n- S031 (Diminished_Thinking): Lately my head feels full of fog—I'll sit down to pay a couple of bills or answer an email and end up staring at the numbers, unable to decide what to do first or even remember what I just read, so everything takes me three times as long.\n- S032 (Death_Suicide): More and more I catch myself thinking my family would be better off if I just didn’t wake up, and on the worst Right before this crash, I went through a stretch where I felt unusually up and wired, talking nonstop about big plans for the new boutique shop and getting snappy and irritated with my wife or the two or three in the morning rearranging tools, sketching a dozen new furniture lines at once, and not feeling tired even though I barely slept.\n- S035 (Inflated_Self_Esteem): In those weeks when I was redesigning everything, I honestly thought I was going to turn this little workshop into some famous online brand and show all the younger guys how it’s really done, like I had some special gift nobody else in town could touch.\n- S039 (Manic_Distractibility): Back when I was on that high, I couldn’t stick with anything—I'd be halfway through cutting a board, then a song on the radio or a message on my phone would pull me away, and pretty soon I’d have five different projects and a dozen tabs open on the computer, none of them finished.\n- S040 (Increased_Goal_Activity): I threw myself into the business like a madman for a while—calling old clients out of the blue, posting nonstop on social media, planning workshops and new product lines all at once, pacing around the shop because I couldn’t sit still long enough to actually complete the orders I already had.\n▸ Additional Diagnostic Requirements\n- functional_impairment: In the past couple of months I’ve fallen behind on I felt incredibly confident, talking nonstop about turning my small workshop into a famous online brand. These high-energy spells followed by heavy crashes have been happening on and off for several years now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least 4 days when you felt unusually energized or irritable, needed much less sleep, and were more talkative or impulsive than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, right before this crash I had a stretch where I was buzzing with energy, barely sleeping, and talking nonstop about my plans. I also got very snappy and irritated with my wife and kids if they asked me to slow down or questioned my ideas. These kinds of revved-up spells have been happening on and off for several years.\n\nDoctor: During those revved-up periods, did you ever have symptoms severe enough to cause major problems at work, need hospitalization, or involve hallucinations or delusional beliefs?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never been hospitalized or experienced things like hallucinations, and my business didn't fall apart while I was in those high moods, even if I was distracted and messy. The real trouble with my work and not being able to function only started once I crashed into this heavy low.\n\nDoctor: Between these mood episodes, have you had at least 2 months at a time when your mood felt mostly normal and you were functioning well, or have the ups and downs been present most of the time for years?\n\nPatient: Yes, over the years these swings have come and gone, and I definitely had stretches of several months in between where I felt like my normal self and could function well. But this current heavy low has been completely different, staying steady and continuous for the last six months without any break."

While I was definitely irritable and scattered, I never had a major breakdown or needed hospitalization during those highs. The real struggle to function and keep up with my life only started once I crashed into this heavy low."

But this current heavy low has been completely different, staying steady and continuous for the last six months without any break.\n\nDoctor: During your revved-up periods, did you ever have to stop working or get urgent help because your behavior became clearly out of control, or were you still able to function without major impairment?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't need urgent help or have to stop working back then; in fact, I felt like I was running on overdrive, staying up until 2 or 3 AM working and planning. While I was definitely irritable and scattered, I never had a major breakdown or needed hospitalization during those highs. The real struggle to function and keep up with my life only started once I crashed into this heavy low.\n\nDoctor: Have your high-energy periods ever lasted at least 4 days and caused clearly noticeable changes in behavior, but without being severe enough to require hospitalization or cause major impairment at work or home?\n\nPatient: Yes, those high-energy periods always lasted for a few weeks at a time, and my family definitely noticed the change because I'd be talking nonstop, staying up until 2 AM, and getting easily irritated. But even though I was scattered and didn't finish projects, it never got bad enough to require hospitalization or cause major problems at work back then. Those severe struggles only started once I crashed into this heavy low."

These kinds of revved-up spells have been happening on and off for several years.\n\nDoctor: During those revved-up periods, did you ever have symptoms severe enough to cause major problems at work, need hospitalization, or involve hallucinations or delusional beliefs?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never been hospitalized or experienced things like hallucinations, and my business didn't fall apart while I was in those high moods, even if I was distracted and messy. The real trouble with my work and not being able to function only started once I crashed into this heavy low.\n\nDoctor: Between these mood episodes, have you had at least 2 months at a time when your mood felt mostly normal and you were functioning well, or have the ups and downs been present most of the time for years?\n\nPatient: Yes, over the years these swings have come and gone, and I definitely had stretches of several months in between where I felt like my normal self and could function well. But this current heavy low has been completely different, staying steady and continuous for the last six months without any break.\n\nDoctor: During your revved-up periods, did you ever have to stop working or get urgent help because your behavior became clearly out of control, or were you still able to function without major impairment?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't need urgent help or have to stop working back then; in fact, I felt like I was running on overdrive, staying up until 2 or 3 AM working and planning. While I was definitely irritable and scattered, I never had a major breakdown or needed hospitalization during those highs. The real struggle to function and keep up with my life only started once I crashed into this heavy low.\n\nDoctor: Have your high-energy periods ever lasted at least 4 days and caused clearly noticeable changes in behavior, but without being severe enough to require hospitalization or cause major impairment at work or home?\n\nPatient: Yes, those high-energy periods always lasted for a few weeks at a time, and my family definitely noticed the change because I'd be talking nonstop, staying up until 2 AM, and getting easily irritated. But even though I was scattered and didn't finish projects, it never got bad enough to require hospitalization or cause major problems at work back then. Those severe struggles only started once I crashed into this heavy low.\n\nDoctor: During your low periods or high-energy periods, have you ever heard or seen things other people couldn’t, or strongly believed things that turned out not to be true?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never seen or heard things that other people couldn't, nor have I held beliefs that were completely out of touch with reality. Even during my worst lows or my highest periods, I've never experienced anything like that."
  }

This heavy low has actually been going on steadily for about six months now, though these kinds of ups and downs have been coming and going for several years.\n\nDoctor: Over the past six months, have you also had at least two weeks at a time when you felt depressed or lost interest in things most of the day nearly every day, even when nothing specific was happening?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has been much longer than two weeks—this heavy, hollow feeling has been with me nearly every day for the last six months. During this time, I just feel empty inside and have completely lost interest in things I used to love, like designing new furniture or going out dancing with my wife.\n\nDoctor: Before these last six months, have you had any earlier periods of similar depression or loss of interest that lasted at least two weeks, or is this the first time it has happened?\n\nPatient: No, this isn't the first time it has happened. These swings between heavy lows and being overly revved up have actually been coming and going for several years. But this current stretch has definitely been the longest and worst of them all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 4 days when you felt unusually energized or overly confident, needed much less sleep, talked more than usual, or did things that were risky or out of character?\n\nPatient: Yes, right before this crash I had a few weeks where I felt unusually up and wired, staying up until 2 or 3 AM working on big plans but never feeling tired. I felt incredibly confident, talking nonstop about turning my small workshop into a famous online brand. These high-energy spells followed by heavy crashes have been happening on and off for several years now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least 4 days when you felt unusually energized or irritable, needed much less sleep, and were more talkative or impulsive than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, right before this crash I had a stretch where I was buzzing with energy, barely sleeping, and talking nonstop about my plans. I also got very snappy and irritated with my wife and kids if they asked me to slow down or questioned my ideas. These kinds of revved-up spells have been happening on and off for several years.\n\nDoctor: During those revved-up periods, did you ever have symptoms severe enough to cause major problems at work, need hospitalization, or involve hallucinations or delusional beliefs?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never been hospitalized or experienced things like hallucinations, and my business didn't fall apart while I was in those high moods, even if I was distracted and messy. The real trouble with my work and not being able to function only started once I crashed into this heavy low.\n\nDoctor: Between these mood episodes, have you had at least 2 months at a time when your mood felt mostly normal and you were functioning well, or have the ups and downs been present most of the time for years?\n\nPatient: Yes, over the years these swings have come and gone, and I definitely had stretches of several months in between where I felt like my normal self and could function well. But this current heavy low has been completely different, staying steady and continuous for the last six months without any break.\n\nDoctor: During your revved-up periods, did you ever have to stop working or get urgent help because your behavior became clearly out of control, or were you still able to function without major impairment?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't need urgent help or have to stop working back then; in fact, I felt like I was running on overdrive, staying up until 2 or 3 AM working and planning. While I was definitely irritable and scattered, I never had a major breakdown or needed hospitalization during those highs. The real struggle to function and keep up with my life only started once I crashed into this heavy low.\n\nDoctor: Have your high-energy periods ever lasted at least 4 days and caused clearly noticeable changes in behavior, but without being severe enough to require hospitalization or cause major impairment at work or home?\n\nPatient: Yes, those high-energy periods always lasted for a few weeks at a time, and my family definitely noticed the change because I'd be talking nonstop, staying up until 2 AM, and getting easily irritated. But even though I was scattered and didn't finish projects, it never got bad enough to require hospitalization or cause major problems at work back then. Those severe struggles only started once I crashed into this heavy low.\n\nDoctor: During your low periods or high-energy periods, have you ever heard or seen things other people couldn’t, or strongly believed things that turned out not to be true?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never seen or heard things that other people couldn't, nor have I held beliefs that were completely out of touch with reality. Even during my worst lows or my highest periods, I've never experienced anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have your mood swings mostly been present for at least 2 years with only brief periods of feeling completely normal, or have there been clear episodes separated by long stable periods?\n\nPatient: These ups and downs have definitely come and gone as distinct swings over several years, separated by long periods where I felt completely normal. But this current heavy low has been the most persistent and worst stretch by far, lasting steadily for about six months now."
  }
]

Even during my worst lows or my highest periods, I've never experienced anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have your mood swings mostly been present for at least 2 years with only brief periods of feeling completely normal, or have there been clear episodes separated by long stable periods?\n\nPatient: These ups and downs have definitely come and gone as distinct swings over several years, separated by long periods where I felt completely normal. However, they never led to hospitalization or total devastation at the time. The severe disruption where I can no longer keep up with my work or life has really only happened during this current low crash."

    "content": "Previous Candidates:\n[\"F31\"]\n\nInterview Transcript:\n\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Lately I keep thinking my family might be better off without me, and those thoughts are starting to scare me. I've never felt this way before, and it's just getting too hard to carry on my own.\n\nDoctor: Did these feelings start after a specific stressful event or life change, and have they been going on for less than 6 months?\n\nPatient: Yes, they got much worse after two of my big commercial contracts unexpectedly ended and my apprentice moved away. This heavy low has actually been going on steadily for about six months now, though these kinds of ups and downs have been coming and going for several years.\n\nDoctor: Over the past six months, have you also had at least two weeks at a time when you felt depressed or lost interest in things most of the day nearly every day, even when nothing specific was happening?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has been much longer than two weeks—this heavy, hollow feeling has been with me nearly every day for the last six months. During this time, I just feel empty inside and have completely lost interest in things I used to love, like designing new furniture or going out dancing with my wife.\n\nDoctor: Before these last six months, have you had any earlier periods of similar depression or loss of interest that lasted at least two weeks, or is this the first time it has happened?\n\nPatient: No, this isn't the first time it has happened. These swings between heavy lows and being overly revved up have actually been coming and going for several years. But this current stretch has definitely been the longest and worst of them all.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 4 days when you felt unusually energized or overly confident, needed much less sleep, talked more than usual, or did things that were risky or out of character?\n\nPatient: Yes, right before this crash I had a few weeks where I felt unusually up and wired, staying up until 2 or 3 AM working on big plans but never feeling tired. I felt incredibly confident, talking nonstop about turning my small workshop into a famous online brand. These high-energy spells followed by heavy crashes have been happening on and off for several years now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting at least 4 days when you felt unusually energized or irritable, needed much less sleep, and were more talkative or impulsive than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, right before this crash I had a stretch where I was buzzing with energy, barely sleeping, and talking nonstop about my plans. I also got very snappy and irritated with my wife and kids if they asked me to slow down or questioned my ideas. These kinds of revved-up spells have been happening on and off for several years.\n\nDoctor: During those revved-up periods, did you ever have symptoms severe enough to cause major problems at work, need hospitalization, or involve hallucinations or delusional beliefs?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never been hospitalized or experienced things like hallucinations, and my business didn't fall apart while I was in those high moods, even if I was distracted and messy. The real trouble with my work and not being able to function only started once I crashed into this heavy low.\n\nDoctor: Between these mood episodes, have you had at least 2 months at a time when your mood felt mostly normal and you were functioning well, or have the ups and downs been present most of the time for years?\n\nPatient: Yes, over the years these swings have come and gone, and I definitely had stretches of several months in between where I felt like my normal self and could function well. But this current heavy low has been completely different, staying steady and continuous for the last six months without any break.\n\nDoctor: During your revved-up periods, did you ever have to stop working or get urgent help because your behavior became clearly out of control, or were you still able to function without major impairment?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't need urgent help or have to stop working back then; in fact, I felt like I was running on overdrive, staying up until 2 or 3 AM working and planning. While I was definitely irritable and scattered, I never had a major breakdown or needed hospitalization during those highs. The real struggle to function and keep up with my life only started once I crashed into this heavy low.\n\nDoctor: Have your high-energy periods ever lasted at least 4 days and caused clearly noticeable changes in behavior, but without being severe enough to require hospitalization or cause major impairment at work or home?\n\nPatient: Yes, those high-energy periods always lasted for a few weeks at a time, and my family definitely noticed the change because I'd be talking nonstop, staying up until 2 AM, and getting easily irritated. But even though I was scattered and didn't finish projects, it never got bad enough to require hospitalization or cause major problems at work back then. Those severe struggles only started once I crashed into this heavy low.\n\nDoctor: During your low periods or high-energy periods, have you ever heard or seen things other people couldn’t, or strongly believed things that turned out not to be true?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never seen or heard things that other people couldn't, nor have I held beliefs that were completely out of touch with reality. Even during my worst lows or my highest periods, I've never experienced anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have your mood swings mostly been present for at least 2 years with only brief periods of feeling completely normal, or have there been clear episodes separated by long stable periods?\n\nPatient: These ups and downs have definitely come and gone as distinct swings over several years, separated by long periods where I felt completely normal. But this current heavy low has been the most persistent and worst stretch by far, lasting steadily for about six months now.\n\nDoctor: During your high-energy periods, did you ever feel so energized or irritable that it caused major problems at work, at home, or led to hospitalization? Or were those periods noticeable but not severely disruptive?\n\nPatient: Those high-energy periods were definitely noticeable and caused some tension, like me getting snappy with my family when they questioned my plans and leaving many projects unfinished in the shop. However, they never led to hospitalization or total devastation at the time. The severe disruption where I can no longer keep up with my work or life has really only happened during this current low crash.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 1 week when your mood was so elevated or irritable that you became clearly out of control, needed hospitalization, or had to stop working because of it?\n\nPatient: No, my up periods never reached the point where I lost control or needed to be hospitalized. During those times, I actually worked excessively and would stay up late in the shop buzzing with energy, even if I was irritable. It is only during these heavy, depressed periods that I have really struggled to keep up with my work and daily life."

The real trouble with my work and not being able to function only started once I crashed into this heavy low.\n\nDoctor: Between these mood episodes, have you had at least 2 months at a time when your mood felt mostly normal and you were functioning well, or have the ups and downs been present most of the time for years?\n\nPatient: Yes, over the years these swings have come and gone, and I definitely had stretches of several months in between where I felt like my normal self and could function well. But this current heavy low has been completely different, staying steady and continuous for the last six months without any break.\n\nDoctor: During your revved-up periods, did you ever have to stop working or get urgent help because your behavior became clearly out of control, or were you still able to function without major impairment?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't need urgent help or have to stop working back then; in fact, I felt like I was running on overdrive, staying up until 2 or 3 AM working and planning. While I was definitely irritable and scattered, I never had a major breakdown or needed hospitalization during those highs. The real struggle to function and keep up with my life only started once I crashed into this heavy low.\n\nDoctor: Have your high-energy periods ever lasted at least 4 days and caused clearly noticeable changes in behavior, but without being severe enough to require hospitalization or cause major impairment at work or home?\n\nPatient: Yes, those high-energy periods always lasted for a few weeks at a time, and my family definitely noticed the change because I'd be talking nonstop, staying up until 2 AM, and getting easily irritated. But even though I was scattered and didn't finish projects, it never got bad enough to require hospitalization or cause major problems at work back then. Those severe struggles only started once I crashed into this heavy low.\n\nDoctor: During your low periods or high-energy periods, have you ever heard or seen things other people couldn’t, or strongly believed things that turned out not to be true?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never seen or heard things that other people couldn't, nor have I held beliefs that were completely out of touch with reality. Even during my worst lows or my highest periods, I've never experienced anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have your mood swings mostly been present for at least 2 years with only brief periods of feeling completely normal, or have there been clear episodes separated by long stable periods?\n\nPatient: These ups and downs have definitely come and gone as distinct swings over several years, separated by long periods where I felt completely normal. But this current heavy low has been the most persistent and worst stretch by far, lasting steadily for about six months now.\n\nDoctor: During your high-energy periods, did you ever feel so energized or irritable that it caused major problems at work, at home, or led to hospitalization? Or were those periods noticeable but not severely disruptive?\n\nPatient: Those high-energy periods were definitely noticeable and caused some tension, like me getting snappy with my family when they questioned my plans and leaving many projects unfinished in the shop. However, they never led to hospitalization or total devastation at the time. The severe disruption where I can no longer keep up with my work or life has really only happened during this current low crash.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 1 week when your mood was so elevated or irritable that you became clearly out of control, needed hospitalization, or had to stop working because of it?\n\nPatient: No, my up periods never reached the point where I lost control or needed to be hospitalized. During those times, I actually worked excessively and would stay up late in the shop buzzing with energy, even if I was irritable. It is only during these heavy, depressed periods that I have really struggled to keep up with my work and daily life.\n\nDoctor: Have your mood symptoms been present most of the time for at least 2 years with only brief periods of feeling normal, or have they occurred as distinct episodes separated by long stable periods?"

I've never felt this way before, and it's just getting too hard to carry on my own.\n\nDoctor: Did these feelings start after a specific stressful event or life change, and have they been going on for less than 6 months?\n\nPatient: Yes, they got much worse after two of my big commercial contracts unexpectedly ended and my apprentice moved away. This heavy low has actually been going on steadily for about six months now, though these kinds of ups and downs have been coming and going for several years.\n\nDoctor: Over the past six months, have you also had at least two weeks at a time when you felt depressed or lost interest in things most of the day nearly every day, even when nothing specific was happening?\n\nPatient: Yes, it has been much longer than two weeks—this heavy, hollow feeling has been with me nearly every day for the last six months. During this time, I just feel empty inside and have completely lost interest in things I used to love, like designing new furniture or going out dancing with my wife.\n\nDoctor: Before these last six months, have you had any earlier periods of similar depression or loss of interest that lasted at least two weeks, or is this the first time it has happened?\n\nPatient: No, this isn't the first time it has happened. I also got very snappy and irritated with my wife and kids if they asked me to slow down or questioned my ideas. The real trouble with my work and not being able to function only started once I crashed into this heavy low.\n\nDoctor: Between these mood episodes, have you had at least 2 months at a time when your mood felt mostly normal and you were functioning well, or have the ups and downs been present most of the time for years?\n\nPatient: Yes, over the years these swings have come and gone, and I definitely had stretches of several months in between where I felt like my normal self and could function well. But this current heavy low has been completely different, staying steady and continuous for the last six months without any break.\n\nDoctor: During your revved-up periods, did you ever have to stop working or get urgent help because your behavior became clearly out of control, or were you still able to function without major impairment?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't need urgent help or have to stop working back then; in fact, I felt like I was running on overdrive, staying up until 2 or 3 AM working and planning. The real struggle to function and keep up with my life only started once I crashed into this heavy low.\n\nDoctor: Have your high-energy periods ever lasted at least 4 days and caused clearly noticeable changes in behavior, but without being severe enough to require hospitalization or cause major impairment at work or home?\n\nPatient: Yes, those high-energy periods always lasted for a few weeks at a time, and my family definitely noticed the change because I'd be talking nonstop, staying up until 2 AM, and getting easily irritated. But even though I was scattered and didn't finish projects, it never got bad enough to require hospitalization or cause major problems at work back then. Those severe struggles only started once I crashed into this heavy low.\n\nDoctor: During your low periods or high-energy periods, have you ever heard or seen things other people couldn’t, or strongly believed things that turned out not to be true?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never seen or heard things that other people couldn't, nor have I held beliefs that were completely out of touch with reality. The severe disruption where I can no longer keep up with my work or life has really only happened during this current low crash.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 1 week when your mood was so elevated or irritable that you became clearly out of control, needed hospitalization, or had to stop working because of it?\n\nPatient: No, my up periods never reached the point where I lost control or needed to be hospitalized. During those times, I actually worked excessively and would stay up late in the shop buzzing with energy, even if I was irritable. It is only during these heavy, depressed periods that I have really struggled to keep up with my work and daily life.\n\nDoctor: Have your mood symptoms been present most of the time for at least 2 years with only brief periods of feeling normal, or have they occurred as distinct episodes separated by long stable periods?\n\nPatient: They have definitely been distinct swings with long stable periods in between over several years, rather than a constant, non-stop feeling. I also got very snappy and irritated with my wife and kids if they asked me to slow down or questioned my ideas. These kinds of revved-up spells have been happening on and off for several years.\n\nDoctor: During those revved-up periods, did you ever have symptoms severe enough to cause major problems at work, need hospitalization, or involve hallucinations or delusional beliefs?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never been hospitalized or experienced things like hallucinations, and my business didn't fall apart while I was in those high moods, even if I was distracted and messy. The real trouble with my work and not being able to function only started once I crashed into this heavy low.\n\nDoctor: Between these mood episodes, have you had at least 2 months at a time when your mood felt mostly normal and you were functioning well, or have the ups and downs been present most of the time for years?\n\nPatient: Yes, over the years these swings have come and gone, and I definitely had stretches of several months in between where I felt like my normal self and could function well. But this current heavy low has been completely different, staying steady and continuous for the last six months without any break.\n\nDoctor: During your revved-up periods, did you ever have to stop working or get urgent help because your behavior became clearly out of control, or were you still able to function without major impairment?\n\nPatient: No, I didn't need urgent help or have to stop working back then; in fact, I felt like I was running on overdrive, staying up until 2 or 3 AM working and planning. While I was definitely irritable and scattered, I never had a major breakdown or needed hospitalization during those highs. The real struggle to function and keep up with my life only started once I crashed into this heavy low.\n\nDoctor: Have your high-energy periods ever lasted at least 4 days and caused clearly noticeable changes in behavior, but without being severe enough to require hospitalization or cause major impairment at work or home?\n\nPatient: Yes, those high-energy periods always lasted for a few weeks at a time, and my family definitely noticed the change because I'd be talking nonstop, staying up until 2 AM, and getting easily irritated. But even though I was scattered and didn't finish projects, it never got bad enough to require hospitalization or cause major problems at work back then. Those severe struggles only started once I crashed into this heavy low.\n\nDoctor: During your low periods or high-energy periods, have you ever heard or seen things other people couldn’t, or strongly believed things that turned out not to be true?\n\nPatient: No, I’ve never seen or heard things that other people couldn't, nor have I held beliefs that were completely out of touch with reality. Even during my worst lows or my highest periods, I've never experienced anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have your mood swings mostly been present for at least 2 years with only brief periods of feeling completely normal, or have there been clear episodes separated by long stable periods?\n\nPatient: These ups and downs have definitely come and gone as distinct swings over several years, separated by long periods where I felt completely normal. But this current heavy low has been the most persistent and worst stretch by far, lasting steadily for about six months now.\n\nDoctor: During your high-energy periods, did you ever feel so energized or irritable that it caused major problems at work, at home, or led to hospitalization? Or were those periods noticeable but not severely disruptive?\n\nPatient: Those high-energy periods were definitely noticeable and caused some tension, like me getting snappy with my family when they questioned my plans and leaving many projects unfinished in the shop. However, they never led to hospitalization or total devastation at the time. The severe disruption where I can no longer keep up with my work or life has really only happened during this current low crash.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 1 week when your mood was so elevated or irritable that you became clearly out of control, needed hospitalization, or had to stop working because of it?\n\nPatient: No, my up periods never reached the point where I lost control or needed to be hospitalized. During those times, I actually worked excessively and would stay up late in the shop buzzing with energy, even if I was irritable. It is only during these heavy, depressed periods that I have really struggled to keep up with my work and daily life.\n\nDoctor: Have your mood symptoms been present most of the time for at least 2 years with only brief periods of feeling normal, or have they occurred as distinct episodes separated by long stable periods?\n\nPatient: They have definitely been distinct swings with long stable periods in between over several years, rather than a constant, non-stop feeling. However, this current heavy and hopeless low has been steady and continuous for about the last six months now.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of at least 2 years where you felt depressed or up-and-down most of the time without long stretches of feeling completely normal in between?\n\nPatient: These swings between feeling overly revved up with big plans and crashing into heavy lows have indeed been coming and going for several years now. However, I've always had long stretches of feeling completely normal in between them, until this current low stretch hit after the contracts fell through. This has been the most severe and continuous one yet.\n\nDoctor: When you think your family might be better off without you, have you had any thoughts of ending your life, or made any plan to hurt yourself?\n\nPatient: Yes, I have, and it really scares me because on the worst nights I’ve found myself picturing hanging myself in the workshop or driving off the cliff road. What terrifies me the most is how calm and reasonable those thoughts are starting to sound to me."
  }
]

Subtypes: Childlike Silliness, Stupor, Catalepsy, Waxy Flexibility, Mutism, Negativism, Posturing, Mannerism, Stereotypy, Agitation, Grimacing, Echolalia, Echopraxia\n[S023] Negative_Symptoms: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.  Subtypes: Diminished Emotional Expression, Avolition, Alogia, Anhedonia, Asociality\n[S024] Depressed_Mood: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.  Subtypes: Depressed Mood, Tearfulness, Sadness, Emptiness, Emotional Numbness\n[S025] Loss_of_Interest: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.  Subtypes: Loss of Interest, Social Withdrawal, Loss of Motivation, Anhedonia, Indifference to Achievement\n[S026] Weight_Appetite_Change: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.  Subtypes: Significant Weight Loss, Weight Gain, Decrease in Appetite, Increase in Appetite\n[S027] Sleep_Disturbance: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep. Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health. Subtypes: Intrusive Memories, Distressing Dreams, Dissociative Reactions (Flashbacks), Psychological Distress at Cues, Physiological Reactions to Cues\n[S070] Trauma_Avoidance: Persistent avoidance of stimuli associated with the traumatic event.  Subtypes: Internal Avoidance, External Avoidance\n[S071] Trauma_Related_Amnesia: Inability to remember an important aspect of the traumatic event (typically due to dissociative amnesia and not to other factors like head injury or alcohol).  Subtypes: Fragmented Memory, Generalized Amnesia\n[S072] Negative_Beliefs: Persistent and exaggerated negative beliefs or expectations about oneself, others, or the world. Subtypes: Self-Blame, Blaming Others\n[S074] Persistent_Negative_Emotional_State: Persistent negative emotional state (e.g., fear, horror, anger, guilt, or shame).  Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others. Subtypes: Anhedonia for Happiness, Lack of Satisfaction, Inability to Feel Love, Emotional Numbness\n[S077] Irritability_Aggression: Irritable behavior and angry outbursts (with little or no provocation) typically expressed as verbal or physical aggression.  Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.
